# Names whose resolution is a known difference from javac's, for `cargo oracle names`. Read by
# tools/oracle-check/src/names/compare.rs; docs/design/semantic-oracle-normalization.md ("Known
# differences") explains each category.
#
# First the categories, each with its explanation:
#
#   category <name>: <explanation>
#
# then the names that show them, by the file as reports name it (its project, then its path) and
# javac's range for the name, in UTF-16 offsets:
#
#   <category> <file> <start>-<end>

category case-label: a `case` label's enum constant in a switch whose selector needs attribution (`switch (x.kind()) { case OPEN: ... }`): which enum it is depends on the selector's type, which is B3's, so B2 leaves the name to B3, or takes a constant of the name in scope that could be a `case` constant, which is wrong when the selector is an enum
category static-import-overload: a method named alone that only static imports have, declared by several imported types (`filter` from both `Sets.*` and `Collections2.*`): javac's searched type is the declaring type of the overload it chooses, and choosing is B3's; B2 gives the first import's type
category patched-platform: a class `--patch-module` adds to a platform module (lucene's java21 sources, patched with the JDK 21 API jar its build extracts): javac's key names the patch jar as the class's origin, while Ferrion's platform serves the patched classes as part of the module, so its key says `platform`
category platform-per-source-set: `ProjectInput::from_project` wraps the platform of each source set compiled with `-source` before release 9 in its own `Configured`, so a dependency's view and the reader's have two platforms, the same class from each is two types, and a member type inherited through both (guava's `Map.Entry`) is ambiguous; to fix by sharing one wrapped platform between source sets with the same platform and options
category jdk-internal-package: guava-tests' tests compile with `-source 8` against JDK 27's own API, where javac finds `sun.security.jca` (a package of java.base it doesn't export); Ferrion's platform for the source set (`PlatformKind::Jdk`) has no such package; to look into in `ferrion_project` and `ferrion_classfile`
category dependency-view: a dependency's type resolved in the dependency's own view (decision 1 of the project symbols review): javac compiles the dependent source set as one namespace, so a type the dependent redeclares replaces the dependency's in the dependency's code too
